  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_2\): The second coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_3\): The third coordinate of ordinary 3-space (also the symmetry
  axis of the fast-flavour analysis). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(f\): The block scale: the common scale factor of the hidden
  direction and the extra times, \(f = \mathrm{e}^{-a_4}b_4^{1/2}\) (the
  block is \(f^2\) times a four-dimensional space of constant curvature
  \(-H^2\)). Unit: dimensionless. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_6\): The second of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_7\): The third of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(b_4\): The author's metric function of the time \(x_4\) in the
  hidden and extra-time components, \(b_4 > 0\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot a_4\): The time derivative \(\mathrm{d}a_4/\mathrm{d}x_4\).
  Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot f\): The time derivative \(\mathrm{d}f/\mathrm{d}x_4\) of the
  block scale. Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot b_4\): The time derivative \(\mathrm{d}b_4/\mathrm{d}x_4\).
  Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot K\): The time derivative \(\mathrm{d}K/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} \(y\) is the proper hidden coordinate in units of
the block scale: in it the hidden direction and the extra times form the
block \(f^2[\mathrm{d}y^2 - \mathrm{e}^{2Hy}(\dots)]\), a
four-dimensional space of constant curvature \(-H^2/f^2\) scaled by one
function \(f\), the block scale. The variable \(K = 1/f^2\) measures the
curvature of the block, \(\mathcal{H}\) is the expansion rate of
ordinary space (the Hubble rate) and \(\mathcal{H}_f\) the rate of
change of the block. One period of \(x_0\) is two halves joined at the
fold \(y = 0\) (where \(\sin^2z = 1\)); each half is closed by the
regular tip \(y = -L\), with \(HL = 3\) (ASSUMED). The last line is the
volume element in both charts. Status: DERIVED (exact change of chart).

\[
\begin{aligned}
&R^{x_1x_2}{}_{x_1x_2} = \mathcal{H}^2,\qquad R^{x_1x_4}{}_{x_1x_4} = \mathcal{H}^2 + \dot{\mathcal{H}},\qquad
 R^{x_1x_0}{}_{x_1x_0} = \mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f,\qquad R^{x_4x_0}{}_{x_4x_0} = \mathcal{H}_f^2 +
 \dot{\mathcal{H}}_f,\\
&R^{x_0x_5}{}_{x_0x_5} = \mathcal{H}_f^2 - H^2K;\\
&\text{the same for every pair of the same kind; all other $R^{ab}{}_{cd}$ with $\{a, b\} \ne \{c, d\}$
 vanish}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-33}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-33).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(R^{MN}{}_{PQ},\ R_{MN},\ R\): The curvature of the metric
  \(g_{MN}\): with four indices the Riemann tensor, with two indices the
  Ricci tensor \(R_{MN} = R^P{}_{MPN}\), without indices the Ricci
  (scalar) curvature \(R = g^{MN}R_{MN}\); \(R^{ab}{}_{ab}\) (no sum) is
  the sectional curvature of the coordinate plane \((a, b)\). Unit:
  1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_2\): The second coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The curvature of the order-0 metric is
pair-diagonal: the only non-zero components of the Riemann tensor are
the sectional curvatures of the coordinate planes, five kinds in all.
\(\mathcal{H}^2\) is the curvature of a plane of ordinary space,
\(\mathcal{H}^2 + \dot{\mathcal{H}}\) that of a space-time plane,
\(\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f\) that of a plane spanned by ordinary space
and a block direction, \(\mathcal{H}_f^2 + \dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\) that of
the plane of the time and a block direction, and
\(\mathcal{H}_f^2 - H^2K\) that of a plane of two block directions,
which contains the constant curvature of the block. None of them depends
on \(x_0\): the metric depends on \(x_0\), its curvature does not.
Status: DERIVED (sympy, identical in both charts; 25 checks of the
geometry).

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathcal{L}_{(1)} = 2\sum_{\text{pairs}}R^{ab}{}_{ab},\qquad
 \mathcal{L}_{(2)} = 8\sum_{\text{2 disjoint pairs}}\ \prod_{\text{pairs}}R^{ab}{}_{ab},\qquad
 \mathcal{L}_{(3)} = 48\sum_{\text{3 disjoint pairs}}\ \prod_{\text{pairs}}R^{ab}{}_{ab},\\
&\mathcal{E}_{(1)}{}^c{}_c = -\sum_{\text{pairs avoiding $c$}}R^{ab}{}_{ab},\qquad
 \mathcal{E}_{(2)}{}^c{}_c = -4\sum_{\text{2 disjoint pairs avoiding
 $c$}}\ \prod_{\text{pairs}}R^{ab}{}_{ab},\\
&\mathcal{E}_{(3)}{}^c{}_c = -24\sum_{\text{3 disjoint pairs avoiding $c$}}\ \prod_{\text{pairs}}R^{ab}{}_{ab}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-34}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-34).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\): The Lovelock density of order \(p\) of the
  metric (a scalar built from \(p\) Riemann tensors):
  \(\mathcal{L}_{(1)} = R\) (Einstein-Hilbert), \(\mathcal{L}_{(2)}\)
  the Gauss-Bonnet density, \(\mathcal{L}_{(3)}\) the cubic Lovelock
  density. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(R^{MN}{}_{PQ},\ R_{MN},\ R\): The curvature of the metric
  \(g_{MN}\): with four indices the Riemann tensor, with two indices the
  Ricci tensor \(R_{MN} = R^P{}_{MPN}\), without indices the Ricci
  (scalar) curvature \(R = g^{MN}R_{MN}\); \(R^{ab}{}_{ab}\) (no sum) is
  the sectional curvature of the coordinate plane \((a, b)\). Unit:
  1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(a\): A frame (orthonormal) index running over \(0, 1, \dots, 7\),
  aligned with \(x_0, \dots, x_7\); raised and lowered with
  \(\eta_{ab}\); a repeated index is summed (except in a sectional
  curvature \(R^{ab}{}_{ab}\)). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(b\): A frame index running over \(0, \dots, 7\) (as \(a\)). Status:
  STANDARD.
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_N\): The Lovelock tensor of order \(p\), the
  metric variation of \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\):
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(1)}\) is the Einstein tensor
  \(R^M{}_N - \frac12\delta^M_NR\); every \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}\) is
  divergence free. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(c\): A frame index running over \(0, \dots, 7\) (as \(a\)). Status:
  STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} For a pair-diagonal curvature the Lovelock density
of order \(p\) is \(2^pp!\) times the sum over all sets of \(p\)
disjoint coordinate pairs of the product of their sectional curvatures,
and the diagonal component \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^c{}_c\) is
\(-2^{p-1}p!\) times the same sum restricted to the pairs that avoid the
direction \(c\). This turns the Lovelock tensors of the order-0 metric
into polynomials of the five curvatures of FE-33. Status: DERIVED
(checked against the Ricci route for \(p = 1\), the explicit
Gauss-Bonnet tensor for \(p = 2\), the Bianchi identities and the
minisuperspace variation with a lapse for \(p = 1, 2, 3\)).

\hypertarget{the-lovelock-tensors-of-the-order-0-metric}{%
\subsection{The Lovelock tensors of the order-0
metric}\label{the-lovelock-tensors-of-the-order-0-metric}}

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{E}_{(1)}{}^{x_4}{}_{x_4} ={}& -3\,\mathcal{H}^{2} - 12\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f -
 6\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2} + 6\,KH^{2} \\
\mathcal{E}_{(1)}{}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} ={}& -3\,\mathcal{H}^{2} - 8\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f -
 10\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - 2\,\dot{\mathcal{H}} - 4\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f + 6\,KH^{2} \\
\mathcal{E}_{(1)}{}^{x_0}{}_{x_0} ={}& -6\,\mathcal{H}^{2} - 9\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f -
 6\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - 3\,\dot{\mathcal{H}} - 3\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f + 3\,KH^{2}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-35}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-35).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_N\): The Lovelock tensor of order \(p\), the
  metric variation of \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\):
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(1)}\) is the Einstein tensor
  \(R^M{}_N - \frac12\delta^M_NR\); every \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}\) is
  divergence free. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The Einstein tensor of the order-0 metric. The
\(x_4\) component contains no time derivative of the rates (it is a
constraint); the \(x_1\) and \(x_0\) components contain
\(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\) and \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). The term \(6KH^2\)
(and \(3KH^2\)) is the curvature of the block. Every off-diagonal
component vanishes, and the \(x_0\) component equals the \(x_5\),
\(x_6\), \(x_7\) components: all four block directions have the same
left side. Status: DERIVED (exact).

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{E}_{(2)}{}^{x_4}{}_{x_4} ={}& -48\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f -
 216\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - 144\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} - 12\,\mathcal{H}_f^{4} +
 72\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{2} \\
 &+ 144\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} + 24\,K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} - 12\,K^{2}H^{4} \\
\mathcal{E}_{(2)}{}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} ={}& -32\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f -
 136\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - 192\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} - 60\,\mathcal{H}_f^{4} \\
 &- 32\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f - 48\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} -
 16\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}^{2} - 96\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f \\
 &- 48\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}_f^{2} + 72\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{2} +
 96\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} + 72\,K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} + 48\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}KH^{2} \\
 &+ 48\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_fKH^{2} - 12\,K^{2}H^{4} \\
\mathcal{E}_{(2)}{}^{x_0}{}_{x_0} ={}& -12\,\mathcal{H}^{4} - 108\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f -
 180\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - 108\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} - 12\,\mathcal{H}_f^{4} \\
 &- 12\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}^{2} - 72\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f -
 36\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - 36\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}^{2} \\
 &- 72\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f - 12\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}_f^{2} +
 72\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{2} + 36\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} + 12\,K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} \\
 &+ 36\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}KH^{2} + 12\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_fKH^{2}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-36}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-36).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_N\): The Lovelock tensor of order \(p\), the
  metric variation of \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\):
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(1)}\) is the Einstein tensor
  \(R^M{}_N - \frac12\delta^M_NR\); every \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}\) is
  divergence free. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The Gauss-Bonnet tensor: products of two sectional
curvatures, so every term is of fourth order in the rates and \(H\)
(with at most one time derivative of a rate). It is weighted by
\(\alpha_{(2)} = H^{-2}/32\) in FE-1. Status: DERIVED (sympy, four
routes).

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{E}_{(3)}{}^{x_4}{}_{x_4} ={}& -1440\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} -
 1080\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{4} + 864\,K\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} +
 1296\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} \\
 &- 216\,K^{2}\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{4} \\
\mathcal{E}_{(3)}{}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} ={}& -576\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} -
 1368\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{4} - 576\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{5} -
 576\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} \\
 &- 144\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}_f^{4} - 864\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} -
 576\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} + 576\,K\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} \\
 &+ 1008\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} + 576\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}H^{2} +
 576\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} \\
 &+ 288\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} + 288\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_fK\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{2} +
 576\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_fK\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} - 216\,K^{2}\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{4} \\
 &- 144\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}K^{2}H^{4} \\
\mathcal{E}_{(3)}{}^{x_0}{}_{x_0} ={}& -648\,\mathcal{H}^{4}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} -
 1224\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} - 648\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{4} -
 648\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} \\
 &- 432\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3} -
 432\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f -
 648\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2} + 216\,K\mathcal{H}^{4}H^{2} \\
 &+ 648\,K\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} + 216\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}H^{2} +
 216\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}K\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{2} + 432\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_fH^{2} \\
 &+ 216\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_fK\mathcal{H}^{2}H^{2}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-37}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-37).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_N\): The Lovelock tensor of order \(p\), the
  metric variation of \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\):
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(1)}\) is the Einstein tensor
  \(R^M{}_N - \frac12\delta^M_NR\); every \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}\) is
  divergence free. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The cubic Lovelock tensor: products of three
sectional curvatures (sixth order), weighted by
\(\alpha_{(3)} = H^{-4}/1152\). In eight dimensions it is the highest
Lovelock order that contributes to the field equations. Status: DERIVED
(sympy, four routes).

\hypertarget{the-background-equations}{%
\subsection{The background equations}\label{the-background-equations}}

On the order-0 metric every source is diagonal,
\(\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4} = -\rho\),
\(\mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = p_3\),
\(\mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0} = p_0\), \(\mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = p_5\)
(FE-2), and the field equations FE-1 reduce to one equation for each
kind of direction:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{p=1}^{3}\alpha_{(p)}\,\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^{x_4}{}_{x_4} + \Lambda = -\kappa\rho,\qquad
 \sum_{p=1}^{3}\alpha_{(p)}\,\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} + \Lambda = \kappa p_3,\\
&\sum_{p=1}^{3}\alpha_{(p)}\,\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^{x_0}{}_{x_0} + \Lambda = \kappa p_0,\qquad
 \sum_{p=1}^{3}\alpha_{(p)}\,\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} + \Lambda = \kappa p_5.
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(p\): The Lovelock order \(p = 1, 2, 3\) (Einstein, Gauss-Bonnet,
  cubic). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\alpha_{(p)}\): The Lovelock coupling of order \(p\),
  \(\alpha_{(p)} = w_{(p)}H^{2-2p}\) (\(\alpha_{(1)} = 1\)). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2-2p}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_N\): The Lovelock tensor of order \(p\), the
  metric variation of \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\):
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(1)}\) is the Einstein tensor
  \(R^M{}_N - \frac12\delta^M_NR\); every \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}\) is
  divergence free. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\Lambda\): The eight-dimensional cosmological constant,
  \(\Lambda = -10H^2\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value: \(-10H^2\).
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_3\): The pressure of ordinary 3-space,
  \(p_3 = \mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = \mathcal{T}^{x_2}{}_{x_2} = \mathcal{T}^{x_3}{}_{x_3}\)
  for an isotropic source. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

With the tensors of FE-35 to FE-37, the author's weights and
\(\Lambda = -10H^2\) they are:

\[
\begin{aligned}
x_4:\quad &-\frac{5\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{4H^{4}} -
 \frac{15\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{16H^{4}} + \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{4H^{2}} \\
 &+ \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{8H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{2H^{2}}
 - \frac{27\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{4H^{2}} \\
 &- \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{2H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{8H^{2}} -
 \frac{3\,K^{2}\mathcal{H}^{2}}{16} + \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}}{4} \\
 &+ \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{2} + \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{4} - 3\,\mathcal{H}^{2} -
 12\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f - 6\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - \frac{3\,K^{2}H^{2}}{8} + 6\,KH^{2} - 10\,H^{2} \\
 &= -\kappa\rho
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-38}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-38).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The constraint: the terms divided by \(H^4\) come
from the cubic Lovelock tensor, those divided by \(H^2\) from the
Gauss-Bonnet tensor, the others from the Einstein tensor; the terms with
\(K\) and \(K^2\) are the curvature of the block entering through the
plane curvatures \(\mathcal{H}_f^2 - H^2K\); \(-10H^2\) is the
cosmological constant. The right side is the total energy density of
every field (FE-3). Status: DERIVED. The residual of the stored static
block in this equation is \(3.9\times10^{-13}\) of its largest term
(from the twelve stored digits of \(K_*\); with the closed form
\(1.2\times10^{-16}\)), and the printed polynomial equals the one in the
code symbolically.

\[
\begin{aligned}
x_1:\quad &\Big[-\frac{\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{2H^{4}} - \frac{\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{8H^{4}} +
 \frac{K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{2H^{2}} + \frac{K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{4H^{2}} \\
 &- \frac{\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{2H^{2}} - \frac{K^{2}}{8} +
 \frac{3\,K}{2} - 2\Big]\,\dot{\mathcal{H}} \\
 &+ \Big[-\frac{3\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{4H^{4}} -
 \frac{\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{2H^{4}} + \frac{K\mathcal{H}^{2}}{4H^{2}} \\
 &+ \frac{K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{2H^{2}} - \frac{\mathcal{H}^{2}}{2H^{2}} -
 \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{2H^{2}} + \frac{3\,K}{2} -
 4\Big]\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f \\
 &-\frac{\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{2H^{4}} -
 \frac{19\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{16H^{4}} - \frac{\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{5}}{2H^{4}} +
 \frac{K\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{2H^{2}} \\
 &+ \frac{7\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{8H^{2}} + \frac{K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{2H^{2}} -
 \frac{\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{H^{2}} - \frac{17\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{4H^{2}} \\
 &- \frac{6\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{H^{2}} - \frac{15\,\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{8H^{2}} -
 \frac{3\,K^{2}\mathcal{H}^{2}}{16} + \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}}{4} \\
 &+ 3\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f + \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{4} - 3\,\mathcal{H}^{2} -
 8\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f - 10\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2} - \frac{3\,K^{2}H^{2}}{8} + 6\,KH^{2} - 10\,H^{2} \\
 &= \kappa p_3
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-39}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-39).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_3\): The pressure of ordinary 3-space,
  \(p_3 = \mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = \mathcal{T}^{x_2}{}_{x_2} = \mathcal{T}^{x_3}{}_{x_3}\)
  for an isotropic source. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The 3-space equation is linear in
\(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\) and \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\) with coefficients that
depend on the rates and on \(K\); it is the equation of motion of the
scale factor of ordinary space, with the pressure \(p_3\) of all matter
along ordinary space as its source. Status: DERIVED. With FE-40 it is
solved for the two rates; the integration from the primordial block to
the static block preserves the constraint FE-38 to \(6\times10^{-10}\).

\[
\begin{aligned}
x_0:\quad &\Big[-\frac{9\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{16H^{4}} -
 \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{8H^{4}} + \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}}{16H^{2}} +
 \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{8H^{2}} \\
 &- \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}^{2}}{8H^{2}} - \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{4H^{2}} -
 \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{8H^{2}} + \frac{9\,K}{8} - 3\Big]\,\dot{\mathcal{H}} \\
 &+ \Big[-\frac{3\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{8H^{4}} -
 \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{16H^{4}} + \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}}{16H^{2}} -
 \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}^{2}}{8H^{2}} \\
 &- \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{4H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{8H^{2}} + \frac{3\,K}{8} -
 3\Big]\,\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f \\
 &-\frac{9\,\mathcal{H}^{4}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{16H^{4}} -
 \frac{17\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{16H^{4}} -
 \frac{9\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{16H^{4}} + \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}^{4}}{16H^{2}} \\
 &+ \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{16H^{2}} +
 \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{16H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}^{4}}{8H^{2}} -
 \frac{27\,\mathcal{H}^{3}\mathcal{H}_f}{8H^{2}} \\
 &- \frac{45\,\mathcal{H}^{2}\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{8H^{2}} -
 \frac{27\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f^{3}}{8H^{2}} - \frac{3\,\mathcal{H}_f^{4}}{8H^{2}} \\
 &+ \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}^{2}}{4} + \frac{9\,K\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f}{8} +
 \frac{3\,K\mathcal{H}_f^{2}}{8} - 6\,\mathcal{H}^{2} - 9\,\mathcal{H}\mathcal{H}_f \\
 &- 6\,\mathcal{H}_f^{2} + 3\,KH^{2} - 10\,H^{2} \\
 &= \kappa p_0;\qquad x_5, x_6, x_7:\ \text{the same left side} = \kappa p_5; \\
 &\text{block-isotropic order-0 metric: the same left side} = \kappa\bar p,\qquad \bar p =
 \tfrac14\big(p_0 + 3p_5\big)
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-40}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-40).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_6\): The second of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_7\): The third of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\bar p\): The hidden pressure, the pressure averaged over the block,
  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The block equation is linear in
\(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\) and \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\) as well; it is the
equation of motion of the block scale. Its source is the pressure along
the block: \(p_0\) for the \(x_0\) component and \(p_5\) for the
\(x_5\), \(x_6\), \(x_7\) components. The left sides of the four block
components are identical, so the order-0 metric (one scale \(f\) for all
four) solves them all only for a block-isotropic source, \(p_0 = p_5\);
in general \(f\) obeys the average with \(\bar p\) (subsection ``The
block pressures'' below). Status: DERIVED. The primordial solutions of
FE-38 to FE-40 (with the stabiliser and radiation) have a one-step
defect of \(1.3\times10^{-11}\) and a constraint residual of
\(3.6\times10^{-12}\) when they are re-integrated from the stored
samples.

\[
\dot\rho = -3\mathcal{H}(\rho + p_3) - \mathcal{H}_f(\rho + p_0) - 3\mathcal{H}_f(\rho + p_5)
 = -3\mathcal{H}(\rho + p_3) - 4\mathcal{H}_f(\rho + \bar p) \tag{FE-41}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-41).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\dot\rho\): The time derivative \(\mathrm{d}\rho/\mathrm{d}x_4\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{9}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(p_3\): The pressure of ordinary 3-space,
  \(p_3 = \mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = \mathcal{T}^{x_2}{}_{x_2} = \mathcal{T}^{x_3}{}_{x_3}\)
  for an isotropic source. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\bar p\): The hidden pressure, the pressure averaged over the block,
  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The conservation law FE-11 on the order-0 metric:
the energy density decreases by the work done by the pressure along each
direction, \(p_3\) along the three directions of ordinary space (rate
\(\mathcal{H}\)), \(p_0\) along the hidden direction and \(p_5\) along
the three extra times (rate \(\mathcal{H}_f\)); the four block terms
combine to \(4\mathcal{H}_f(\rho + \bar p)\). For the stabiliser
(\(p_0 = p_5 = \rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\), \(p_3 = -\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\))
it gives the equation of motion of the stabiliser density

\[
\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}}{\mathrm{d}x_4} = -8\,\mathcal{H}_f\,\rho_{\mathrm{stab}},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad \rho_{\mathrm{stab}} \propto f^{-8}
\] \textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathrm{d}\): The differential: \(\mathrm{d}X\) is an infinitesimal
  change of \(X\), \(\mathrm{d}X/\mathrm{d}Y\) the derivative of \(X\)
  with respect to \(Y\), \(\mathrm{d}^nX\) the volume element of \(n\)
  variables. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\): The energy density of the block stabiliser,
  a function of the block scale,
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} \propto f^{-8}\); its value at the static block
  is \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}(f_*)\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Value:
  \(\kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}(f_*) = (26 + 4\sqrt{51})H^2 = 54.566\,H^2\).
  Status: ASSUMED.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(f\): The block scale: the common scale factor of the hidden
  direction and the extra times, \(f = \mathrm{e}^{-a_4}b_4^{1/2}\) (the
  block is \(f^2\) times a four-dimensional space of constant curvature
  \(-H^2\)). Unit: dimensionless. Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

as assumed in IN-4. Status: DERIVED.

\hypertarget{the-block-pressures}{%
\subsection{The block pressures}\label{the-block-pressures}}

The block scale \(f\) multiplies the four block directions together, so
the action depends on it through the sum of the four block components.
For a change \(\Delta\ln f\) of the block scale
(\(\Delta g^{MM} = -2g^{MM}\Delta\ln f\) for the four block directions),
the change of the action is the sum of the four block equations, and
because their left sides are equal,

\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{\Delta S}{\Delta\ln f} &= -\frac{1}{\kappa}\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\sum_{M\in\{x_0,\,x_5,\,x_6,\,x_7\}}
 \Big(\sum_{p=1}^{3}\alpha_{(p)}\,\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_M + \Lambda - \kappa\,\mathcal{T}^M{}_M\Big)\\
 &= -\frac{4}{\kappa}\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\,\Big(\sum_{p=1}^{3}\alpha_{(p)}\,\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^{x_0}{}_{x_0} + \Lambda -
 \kappa\bar p\Big),\qquad \bar p = \tfrac14\big(p_0 + 3p_5\big).
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\Delta\): The change (difference or small perturbation) of the
  quantity that follows: \(\Delta K\), \(\Delta\rho\), \(\Delta p_3\)
  are first-order changes about the static block,
  \(\Delta\varepsilon_o\) a level shift,
  \(\Delta m^2_{21} = m_2^2 - m_1^2\) a difference of squared masses,
  \(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}}\) an addition to \(N_{\mathrm{eff}}\),
  \(\Delta S_{\mathrm{m}}\) the change of an action under a change
  \(\Delta g^{MN}\) of the metric. In an uncertainty relation it is the
  spread, the root-mean-square deviation from the mean value in a state:
  \(\Delta x_0\), \(\Delta k_{x_0}\), \(\Delta\xi\), \(\Delta(fy)\) (as
  in the author's \(\Delta x_0\,\Delta p_{x_0} \ge \hbar/2\)). Status:
  STANDARD.
\item
  \(S\): The action of UM-2 (the integral of the Lagrangian density over
  spacetime); its variation with respect to every field gives the field
  equations. Unit: dimensionless (\(\hbar = 1\)). Status: ASSUMED.
\item
  \(f\): The block scale: the common scale factor of the hidden
  direction and the extra times, \(f = \mathrm{e}^{-a_4}b_4^{1/2}\) (the
  block is \(f^2\) times a four-dimensional space of constant curvature
  \(-H^2\)). Unit: dimensionless. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(g_{MN}\): The metric tensor of the eight-dimensional spacetime
  (signature \(+,+,+,+,-,-,-,-\) in the order \(x_0, \dots, x_7\)):
  \(g_{MN}\,\mathrm{d}X^M\mathrm{d}X^N\) is the squared interval;
  \(g^{MN}\) is its inverse, \(g^M{}_N = \delta^M_N\), and
  \(\lvert g\rvert\) the absolute value of its determinant, so that
  \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\,\mathrm{d}^8X\) is the proper volume element.
  Unit: dimensionless. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(M\): A coordinate index of eight dimensions (also with a numbered
  copy \(M_1, M_2, \dots\)): it runs over \(x_0, x_1, \dots, x_7\); a
  repeated index is summed. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_6\): The second of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_7\): The third of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p\): The Lovelock order \(p = 1, 2, 3\) (Einstein, Gauss-Bonnet,
  cubic). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\alpha_{(p)}\): The Lovelock coupling of order \(p\),
  \(\alpha_{(p)} = w_{(p)}H^{2-2p}\) (\(\alpha_{(1)} = 1\)). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2-2p}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}{}^M{}_N\): The Lovelock tensor of order \(p\), the
  metric variation of \(\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}\mathcal{L}_{(p)}\):
  \(\mathcal{E}_{(1)}\) is the Einstein tensor
  \(R^M{}_N - \frac12\delta^M_NR\); every \(\mathcal{E}_{(p)}\) is
  divergence free. Unit: 1/\(\mathrm{length}^{2p}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\Lambda\): The eight-dimensional cosmological constant,
  \(\Lambda = -10H^2\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value: \(-10H^2\).
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{T}^M{}_N\): The energy-momentum tensor of all matter (every
  field except gravity, the stabiliser included), the source of gravity;
  defined by the change of the matter action under a change of the
  metric. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\bar p\): The hidden pressure, the pressure averaged over the block,
  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

So the equation of motion of \(f\) is the block equation FE-40 with the
hidden pressure \(\bar p\), the average of the block pressures. The
difference of the block pressures,

\[
p_0 = \bar p + \tfrac34\big(p_0 - p_5\big),\qquad p_5 = \bar p - \tfrac14\big(p_0 - p_5\big),
\]

\textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\bar p\): The hidden pressure, the pressure averaged over the block,
  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

cannot be absorbed by \(f\): it would change the shape of the block (the
proper length of the hidden direction against that of the extra times),
a mode outside the order-0 metric. This block-shape mode and its
response to \(p_0 - p_5\) are not computed in UM-2 (OPEN). The
physically correct block equations are therefore: the \(x_0\) component
with \(p_0\), the extra-time components with \(p_5\), and, for the
order-0 metric, their average with \(\bar p\).

The sources of UM-2 are:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the stabiliser: block-isotropic, \(p_0 = p_5 = \rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\),
  so \(\bar p = p_0 = p_5\) and its static block (FE-42, FE-43) is an
  exact solution of all eight components;
\item
  the Standard-Model plasma and matter, and the dark matter: the masses
  of the zero modes and of the dark matter depend on the proper length
  of the hidden direction only (they are \(x_0\) eigenvalues), so their
  mass terms contribute to \(p_0\) only,
  \(p_0 = -(\rho - 3p_3)\,\mathrm{d}\ln m/\mathrm{d}\ln f\) for an ideal
  gas, and nothing to \(p_5\) for quanta without momentum along the
  extra times; the parts of the plasma pressure that depend on the gauge
  couplings (the four-dimensional couplings scale with the volume
  \(V_\perp\) of all four block directions, FE-82, and with them the QCD
  scale, FE-84) contribute to \(p_0\) and \(p_5\) alike; for the dark
  matter at rest
  \(p_0/\rho = -\mathrm{d}\ln\varepsilon_{\mathrm{DM}}/\mathrm{d}\ln f = 0.772034\)
  and \(p_5 = 0\), so \(\bar p = p_0/4\) and
  \(\lvert p_0 - p_5\rvert/\bar p = 4\);
\item
  the Kohn-Sham states of dirac16complex: \(p_0\) and \(p_5\) from
  FE-67; for the self-interacting test states
  \(\lvert p_0 - p_5\rvert/\bar p = 0.90\).
\end{itemize}

The block-shape source is therefore not small compared with the hidden
pressure that is kept; its absolute size is suppressed like that of
\(\bar p\), at most \(3\times10^{-32}\) of the stabiliser (at the
dark-matter freeze-out), if the shape mode is stable (OPEN).

\textbf{What the code does.} The background code
\texttt{um2\_background.py} writes the block equation once, for
``\(x_0 = x_5 = x_6 = x_7\)'', with one symbol for the block pressure;
it is used only for block-isotropic sources: the stabiliser
(\(p_0 = p_5 = \rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\); and the stability family
\(p_0 = p_5 = \nu\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\), \(\nu = \frac14\) to 3), for
which that symbol equals \(p_0\), \(p_5\) and \(\bar p\) alike. Its
first-order response to matter (FE-45 to FE-47) is written for
\(\Delta\bar p = \frac14(\Delta p_0 + 3\Delta p_5)\). The Kohn-Sham job
\texttt{um2\_ks\_jobs.py} (feedback) forms
\(\bar p = \frac14(p_0 + 3p_5)\) from the cell averages of FE-67 and
stores \(p_0 - p_5\) separately; the coupled solution
\texttt{um2\_coupled.py} uses
\(\bar p = \frac14\,\mathrm{d}p/\mathrm{d}\ln f\) at fixed temperature
(FE-81), which is \(\frac14(p_0 + 3p_5)\) for every source, because a
change of \(\ln f\) scales the four block directions together (for the
mass terms \(p_5 = 0\) and it is \(p_0/4\)); and the residual analysis
\texttt{um2\_residuals.py} recomputes the response with
\(\bar p = \frac14(p_0 + 3p_5)\) and reports the block-shape source. No
stored number rests on an inconsistent choice of the block pressure.
Version 3 of the UM-2 documents wrote \(\kappa\bar p\) in the block
equation of the static block and the stabiliser, and in one place
\(\kappa p_0\): both are correct there, because the stabiliser is
block-isotropic, and \(K_\pm\), \(\mathcal{G}(K_*)\),
\(\kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}(f_*)\) and \(\omega_{\mathrm{r}}\) do not
depend on the choice.

\hypertarget{the-static-block-and-the-stabiliser}{%
\subsection{The static block and the
stabiliser}\label{the-static-block-and-the-stabiliser}}

A static block has \(\mathcal{H}_f = 0\) and
\(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f = 0\). With \(\dot{\mathcal{H}} = 0\) the
background equations become:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&x_4:\ -3\mathcal{G}(K)\,\mathcal{H}^2 + 6H^2K\big(1 - 2w_{(2)}K\big) + \Lambda + \kappa\rho = 0,\qquad
 x_1:\ \text{the same with } -\kappa p_3 \text{ in place of } \kappa\rho,\\
&x_0:\ 6\big(12w_{(2)}K - 1\big)\mathcal{H}^2 - \frac{12\big(w_{(2)} -
 18w_{(3)}K\big)}{H^2}\,\mathcal{H}^4 + 3H^2K + \Lambda
 - \kappa p_0 = 0,\\
&x_5, x_6, x_7:\ \text{the same with } \kappa p_5 \text{ in place of } \kappa p_0;\qquad
 \text{block-isotropic order-0 metric: the same with } \kappa\bar p,\\
&\mathcal{G}(K) = 1 - 24w_{(2)}K + 72w_{(3)}K^2 = \frac{K^2 - 12K + 16}{16}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-42}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-42).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathcal{G}(K)\): The coefficient of the four-dimensional
  Einstein-Hilbert term on a static block,
  \(\mathcal{G}(K) = 1 - 24w_{(2)}K + 72w_{(3)}K^2 = (K^2 - 12K + 16)/16\);
  four-dimensional gravity is attractive for \(\mathcal{G} > 0\). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value:
  \(\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 20/3 + \sqrt{51} = 13.8081\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(w_{(p)}\): The author's Lovelock weights, \(w_{(1)} = 1\),
  \(w_{(2)} = 1/32\), \(w_{(3)} = 1/1152\). Unit: dimensionless. Value:
  \((1, 1/32, 1/1152)\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\Lambda\): The eight-dimensional cosmological constant,
  \(\Lambda = -10H^2\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value: \(-10H^2\).
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_1\): The first coordinate of ordinary 3-space. Unit: length.
  Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_3\): The pressure of ordinary 3-space,
  \(p_3 = \mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = \mathcal{T}^{x_2}{}_{x_2} = \mathcal{T}^{x_3}{}_{x_3}\)
  for an isotropic source. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_6\): The second of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_7\): The third of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\bar p\): The hidden pressure, the pressure averaged over the block,
  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} \(\mathcal{G}(K)\) multiplies \(\mathcal{H}^2\) in
the constraint; it is the coefficient of the four-dimensional
Einstein-Hilbert term (FE-48), so four-dimensional gravity is attractive
where \(\mathcal{G} > 0\), i.e.~for \(K < 6 - 2\sqrt5\) or
\(K > 6 + 2\sqrt5 = 10.47\). The term \(6H^2K(1 - 2w_{(2)}K)\) is the
curvature energy of the block (Einstein and Gauss-Bonnet); in the block
equation \(3H^2K\). The sources are \(\kappa\rho\) (constraint),
\(\kappa p_3\) (3-space), \(\kappa p_0\) (hidden direction) and
\(\kappa p_5\) (extra times). Without sources and with
\(\mathcal{H} = 0\) the only static block is \(K = 8\), which would need
\(\Lambda = -24H^2\) (not the author's \(-10H^2\)) and has
\(\mathcal{G} = -1\), repulsive gravity: with the author's weights the
block must be held by a source. Status: DERIVED (exact).

At \(\mathcal{H} = 0\) the constraint and the block equation read

\[
x_4:\ 6H^2K - \tfrac38H^2K^2 - 10H^2 = -\kappa\rho,\qquad x_0:\ 3H^2K - 10H^2 = \kappa p_0,
\]

\textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

and with the stabiliser as the only source
(\(\rho = p_0 = p_5 = \rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\),
\(p_3 = -\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\), FE-4) they require

\[
10H^2 - 6H^2K + \tfrac38H^2K^2 = 3H^2K - 10H^2\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad 3K^2 - 72K + 160 = 0\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad
K = 12 \pm \tfrac43\sqrt{51}:
\]

\textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} = 10H^2 - 6H^2K + \tfrac38H^2K^2\ \ (x_4),\qquad
 \kappa p_0 = \kappa p_5 = \kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} = 3H^2K - 10H^2\ \ (x_0;\ x_5, x_6, x_7),\\
&\Rightarrow\ K_{\pm} = 12 \pm \tfrac43\sqrt{51},\qquad
K_* = 12 + \tfrac43\sqrt{51} = 21.5219,\qquad f_* = K_*^{-1/2} = 0.215556,\\
&\mathcal{G}(K_*) = \tfrac{20}{3} + \sqrt{51} = 13.8081,\qquad \kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}(f_*) = \big(26
 + 4\sqrt{51}\big)H^2
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-43}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-43).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\): The energy density of the block stabiliser,
  a function of the block scale,
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} \propto f^{-8}\); its value at the static block
  is \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}(f_*)\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Value:
  \(\kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}(f_*) = (26 + 4\sqrt{51})H^2 = 54.566\,H^2\).
  Status: ASSUMED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_0\): The pressure along the hidden direction \(x_0\),
  \(p_0 = \mathcal{T}^{x_0}{}_{x_0}\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\).
  Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(p_5\): The pressure along the extra times,
  \(p_5 = \mathcal{T}^{x_5}{}_{x_5} = \mathcal{T}^{x_6}{}_{x_6} = \mathcal{T}^{x_7}{}_{x_7}\).
  Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(x_0\): The hidden space-like coordinate (listed first); the metric
  depends on it through \(z = 6Hx_0\), periodically with the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\). Unit: length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_5\): The first of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_6\): The second of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_7\): The third of the three extra time-like coordinates. Unit:
  length. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(K_\pm\): The two static blocks with the stabiliser,
  \(K_\pm = 12 \pm \frac43\sqrt{51}\). Unit: dimensionless. Value:
  21.5219 and 2.4781. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K_*\): The block variable of the stabilised block,
  \(K_* = 12 + \frac43\sqrt{51}\) (the upper root \(K_+\)). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value: 21.5219. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(f_*\): The block scale of the stabilised (static) block,
  \(f_* = K_*^{-1/2}\). Unit: dimensionless. Value: 0.215556. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{G}(K)\): The coefficient of the four-dimensional
  Einstein-Hilbert term on a static block,
  \(\mathcal{G}(K) = 1 - 24w_{(2)}K + 72w_{(3)}K^2 = (K^2 - 12K + 16)/16\);
  four-dimensional gravity is attractive for \(\mathcal{G} > 0\). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value:
  \(\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 20/3 + \sqrt{51} = 13.8081\). Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The first line gives the stabiliser density required
by the constraint and by the block equations; the stabiliser is
block-isotropic, so the \(x_0\) and the extra-time equations coincide
and the static block is an exact solution of all components (the 3-space
equation is the constraint with
\(-\kappa p_3 = \kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\)). Of the two roots, \(K_*\)
(upper) is the vacuum of UM-2: four-dimensional gravity is attractive
there (\(\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 13.8081\)) and the stabiliser energy is
positive, \(\kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} = 54.566\,H^2\); the lower root
\(K_- = 2.4781\) has \(\mathcal{G} = -0.4748\). The block scale
\(f_* = 0.215556\) fixes the proper size of the hidden direction,
\(f_*L = 3f_*/H = 8.926\times10^{-21}\) m on each half. Status: DERIVED
(closed form; the stabiliser itself ASSUMED).

\hypertarget{the-radion-and-the-response-of-the-block-to-matter}{%
\subsection{The radion and the response of the block to
matter}\label{the-radion-and-the-response-of-the-block-to-matter}}

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}} = \Xi_{\mathcal{H}K}\,\Delta K,\qquad \Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f =
 \Xi_{fK}\,\Delta K,\qquad
 \Delta\dot K = -2K_*\,\Delta\mathcal{H}_f,\\
&\omega_{\mathrm{r}}^2 = 2K_*\,\Xi_{fK} = \Big(\frac{31552}{2249} - \frac{2416\sqrt{51}}{6747}\Big)H^2 =
 (3.38705\,H)^2
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-44}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-44).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\Delta\): The change (difference or small perturbation) of the
  quantity that follows: \(\Delta K\), \(\Delta\rho\), \(\Delta p_3\)
  are first-order changes about the static block,
  \(\Delta\varepsilon_o\) a level shift,
  \(\Delta m^2_{21} = m_2^2 - m_1^2\) a difference of squared masses,
  \(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}}\) an addition to \(N_{\mathrm{eff}}\),
  \(\Delta S_{\mathrm{m}}\) the change of an action under a change
  \(\Delta g^{MN}\) of the metric. In an uncertainty relation it is the
  spread, the root-mean-square deviation from the mean value in a state:
  \(\Delta x_0\), \(\Delta k_{x_0}\), \(\Delta\xi\), \(\Delta(fy)\) (as
  in the author's \(\Delta x_0\,\Delta p_{x_0} \ge \hbar/2\)). Status:
  STANDARD.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}K}\): The coefficient of \(\Delta K\) in the
  first-order equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\) (linearised block
  about \(K_*\)). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value:
  \(6(238 - 19\sqrt{51})H^2/2249 = 0.272956\,H^2\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{fK}\): The coefficient of \(\Delta K\) in the first-order
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value:
  \((4063 - 485\sqrt{51})H^2/2249 = 0.266522\,H^2\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot K\): The time derivative \(\mathrm{d}K/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K_*\): The block variable of the stabilised block,
  \(K_* = 12 + \frac43\sqrt{51}\) (the upper root \(K_+\)). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value: 21.5219. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}_f\): The expansion rate of the block,
  \(\mathcal{H}_f = \dot f/f = \dot b_4/(2b_4) - \dot a_4\) (negative
  when the extra times and \(x_0\) deflate). Unit: GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\omega_{\mathrm{r}}\): The angular frequency of the oscillation of
  the block about the static point (the radion), equal to the radion
  mass. Unit: GeV. Value: \(3.38705\,H = 48.42\) TeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} Linearising FE-38 to FE-40 about the stabilised
block (with \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} \propto K^4\), from FE-41) gives a
closed linear system for the changes of the two rates and of \(K\):
\(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}K}\) and \(\Xi_{fK}\) are the restoring coefficients,
and \(\Delta\dot K = -2K_*\Delta\mathcal{H}_f\) is the definition of
\(\mathcal{H}_f\). The block oscillates with the frequency
\(\omega_{\mathrm{r}}\): the radion, the quantum of the block scale,
with the mass
\(m_{\mathrm{r}} = \omega_{\mathrm{r}} = 3.38705\,H = 48.42\) TeV. The
eigenvalues are 0 and \(\pm\mathrm{i}\omega_{\mathrm{r}}\): the block is
stable, and it is stable for every block-isotropic stabiliser law
\(p_0 = p_5 = \nu\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\) with \(\nu = \frac14\) to 3
(\(\omega_{\mathrm{r}} = 3.33\) to \(4.10\,H\)). The full nonlinear
evolution gives \(3.3868\,H\) (linear and nonlinear frequencies agree to
\(7.9\times10^{-5}\)). Status: DERIVED.

\[
\begin{aligned}
\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}} &= \Xi_{\mathcal{H}K}\,\Delta K + \Xi_{\mathcal{H}3}\,\kappa\Delta p_3
 + \Xi_{\mathcal{H}p}\,\kappa\Delta\bar p + \Xi_{\mathcal{H}\rho}\,\kappa\Delta\rho,\qquad
 3\mathcal{G}(K_*)\,\mathcal{H}^2 = \kappa\Delta\rho,\\
\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f &= \Xi_{fK}\,\Delta K + \Xi_{f3}\,\kappa\Delta p_3 + \Xi_{fp}\,\kappa\Delta\bar p
 + \Xi_{f\rho}\,\kappa\Delta\rho,\\
\Delta\ddot K + \omega_{\mathrm{r}}^2\,\Delta K &= -2K_*\big(\Xi_{f3}\,\kappa\Delta p_3 +
 \Xi_{fp}\,\kappa\Delta\bar p
 + \Xi_{f\rho}\,\kappa\Delta\rho\big)
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-45}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-45).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\Delta\): The change (difference or small perturbation) of the
  quantity that follows: \(\Delta K\), \(\Delta\rho\), \(\Delta p_3\)
  are first-order changes about the static block,
  \(\Delta\varepsilon_o\) a level shift,
  \(\Delta m^2_{21} = m_2^2 - m_1^2\) a difference of squared masses,
  \(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}}\) an addition to \(N_{\mathrm{eff}}\),
  \(\Delta S_{\mathrm{m}}\) the change of an action under a change
  \(\Delta g^{MN}\) of the metric. In an uncertainty relation it is the
  spread, the root-mean-square deviation from the mean value in a state:
  \(\Delta x_0\), \(\Delta k_{x_0}\), \(\Delta\xi\), \(\Delta(fy)\) (as
  in the author's \(\Delta x_0\,\Delta p_{x_0} \ge \hbar/2\)). Status:
  STANDARD.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}K}\): The coefficient of \(\Delta K\) in the
  first-order equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\) (linearised block
  about \(K_*\)). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value:
  \(6(238 - 19\sqrt{51})H^2/2249 = 0.272956\,H^2\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}3}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta p_3\) in the
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\). Unit: dimensionless. Value:
  \(2283/4498 - 162\sqrt{51}/2249 = -0.006853\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(p_3\): The pressure of ordinary 3-space,
  \(p_3 = \mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = \mathcal{T}^{x_2}{}_{x_2} = \mathcal{T}^{x_3}{}_{x_3}\)
  for an isotropic source. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}p}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\bar p\) in
  the equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \((28\sqrt{51} - 114)/2249 = 0.038221\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\bar p\): The hidden pressure, the pressure averaged over the block,
  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}\rho}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\rho\) in
  the equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \(135021/265382 - 10308\sqrt{51}/132691 = -0.045997\). Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\mathcal{G}(K)\): The coefficient of the four-dimensional
  Einstein-Hilbert term on a static block,
  \(\mathcal{G}(K) = 1 - 24w_{(2)}K + 72w_{(3)}K^2 = (K^2 - 12K + 16)/16\);
  four-dimensional gravity is attractive for \(\mathcal{G} > 0\). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value:
  \(\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 20/3 + \sqrt{51} = 13.8081\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(K_*\): The block variable of the stabilised block,
  \(K_* = 12 + \frac43\sqrt{51}\) (the upper root \(K_+\)). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value: 21.5219. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\): The time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}_f/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\).
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{fK}\): The coefficient of \(\Delta K\) in the first-order
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Value:
  \((4063 - 485\sqrt{51})H^2/2249 = 0.266522\,H^2\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{f3}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta p_3\) in the equation
  for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless. Value:
  \(21\sqrt{51}/2249 - 171/4498 = 0.028666\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{fp}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\bar p\) in the
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \(239\sqrt{51}/6747 - 485/2249 = 0.037320\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{f\rho}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\rho\) in the
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \(57/4498 - 7\sqrt{51}/2249 = -0.009555\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\ddot K\): The second time derivative
  \(\mathrm{d}^2K/\mathrm{d}x_4^2\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{2}\). Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\omega_{\mathrm{r}}\): The angular frequency of the oscillation of
  the block about the static point (the radion), equal to the radion
  mass. Unit: GeV. Value: \(3.38705\,H = 48.42\) TeV. Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} Matter on top of the stabiliser changes the sources
by \(\kappa\Delta\rho\), \(\kappa\Delta p_3\) and \(\kappa\Delta\bar p\)
(the block-averaged changes; the shape source
\(\Delta p_0 - \Delta p_5\) is outside the order-0 metric). Each enters
both rates with the coefficients of FE-46; \(\mathcal{H}^2\) is of first
order and is fixed by the constraint,
\(3\mathcal{G}(K_*)\mathcal{H}^2 = \kappa\Delta\rho\). The last line is
the radion equation with matter as its source. Status: DERIVED (exact to
first order). The residual of the adiabatic solution in the full
nonlinear equations is \(1.4\times10^{-7}\) for sources of
\(10^{-5}\,H^2\) and falls by the factor 10 with the sources (it is of
second order).

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\Xi_{\mathcal{H}K} = \frac{6\big(238 - 19\sqrt{51}\big)}{2249}H^2,\qquad \Xi_{fK} = \frac{4063 -
 485\sqrt{51}}{2249}H^2,\qquad
 \Xi_{\mathcal{H}3} = \frac{2283}{4498} - \frac{162\sqrt{51}}{2249},\\
&\Xi_{\mathcal{H}p} = \frac{28\sqrt{51} - 114}{2249},\qquad
 \Xi_{\mathcal{H}\rho} = \frac{135021}{265382} - \frac{10308\sqrt{51}}{132691},\qquad
 \Xi_{f3} = \frac{21\sqrt{51}}{2249} - \frac{171}{4498},\\
&\Xi_{fp} = \frac{239\sqrt{51}}{6747} - \frac{485}{2249},\qquad \Xi_{f\rho} = \frac{57}{4498} -
 \frac{7\sqrt{51}}{2249}
\end{aligned} \tag{FE-46}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-46).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}K}\): The coefficient of \(\Delta K\) in the
  first-order equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}\) (linearised block

  \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}\rho}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\rho\) in

\item
  \(\Xi_{f3}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta p_3\) in the equation

  \(21\sqrt{51}/2249 - 171/4498 = 0.028666\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{fp}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\bar p\) in the
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \(239\sqrt{51}/6747 - 485/2249 = 0.037320\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{f\rho}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\rho\) in the
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \(57/4498 - 7\sqrt{51}/2249 = -0.009555\). Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The exact coefficients (rational numbers and
\(\sqrt{51}\), from \(K_* = 12 + \frac43\sqrt{51}\)): numerically
\(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}K} = 0.272956\,H^2\), \(\Xi_{fK} = 0.266522\,H^2\),
\(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}3} = -0.006853\), \(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}p} = 0.038221\),
\(\Xi_{\mathcal{H}\rho} = -0.045997\), \(\Xi_{f3} = 0.028666\),
\(\Xi_{fp} = 0.037320\), \(\Xi_{f\rho} = -0.009555\). Status: DERIVED
(sympy).

For a source that changes slowly compared with \(1/\omega_{\mathrm{r}}\)
the radion follows it adiabatically, \(\Delta\ddot K = 0\):

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\Delta K = -\frac{2K_*}{\omega_{\mathrm{r}}^2}\big(\Xi_{f3}\,\kappa\Delta p_3 + \Xi_{fp}\,\kappa\Delta\bar p +
 \Xi_{f\rho}\,\kappa\Delta\rho\big):\\
&c_3 = -\frac{2K_*\Xi_{f3}H^2}{\omega_{\mathrm{r}}^2},\qquad c_p = -\frac{2K_*\Xi_{fp}H^2}{\omega_{\mathrm{r}}^2},\qquad
 c_\rho = -\frac{2K_*\Xi_{f\rho}H^2}{\omega_{\mathrm{r}}^2}.
\end{aligned}
\]

  \(p_3 = \mathcal{T}^{x_1}{}_{x_1} = \mathcal{T}^{x_2}{}_{x_2} = \mathcal{T}^{x_3}{}_{x_3}\)

\item

  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless.

\item

  \(\bar p = (p_0 + 3p_5)/4\); for a species at fixed temperature
  \(\bar p = \frac14\,\partial p/\partial\ln f\). Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (or \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) for a four-dimensional
  density). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Xi_{f\rho}\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\rho\) in the
  equation for \(\Delta\dot{\mathcal{H}}_f\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: \(57/4498 - 7\sqrt{51}/2249 = -0.009555\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\rho\): An energy density: in eight dimensions
  \(\rho = -\mathcal{T}^{x_4}{}_{x_4}\) (of the total source, or of the
  species named by a subscript; \(\rho_s\) of the species \(s\),
  \(\rho_i\) of the neutrino mass state \(i\)); in the cosmological
  perturbation equations \(\Delta\rho\) is the density perturbation
  summed over the species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{8}\) (eight
  dimensions), \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\) (four dimensions). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(c_3\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta p_3\) in the adiabatic
  block response, \(c_3 = -3c_\rho\) (so radiation drops out). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value: -0.107556. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(c_p\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\bar p\) in the adiabatic
  block response. Unit: dimensionless. Value: -0.140028. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(c_\rho\): The coefficient of \(\kappa\Delta\rho\) in the adiabatic
  block response \(\Delta K\). Unit: dimensionless. Value: 0.0358521.
  Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

The hidden-pressure coefficient can be read off the block equation
directly: with \(\Delta\rho = \Delta p_3 = 0\) (so \(\mathcal{H} = 0\))
the block equation at \(K_* + \Delta K\) with the stabiliser
\(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} \propto K^4\) and the extra hidden pressure gives

\[
3H^2\Delta K - \kappa\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}}{\mathrm{d}K}\,\Delta K = \kappa\Delta\bar p,\qquad
\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}}{\mathrm{d}K} = \frac{4\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}}{K}\quad\Rightarrow\quad
c_p = \frac{H^2}{3H^2 - 4\kappa\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}/K_*} = -0.140028,
\]

\textbf{Symbols in this equation.}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\Delta\): The change (difference or small perturbation) of the
  quantity that follows: \(\Delta K\), \(\Delta\rho\), \(\Delta p_3\)
  are first-order changes about the static block,
  \(\Delta\varepsilon_o\) a level shift,
  \(\Delta m^2_{21} = m_2^2 - m_1^2\) a difference of squared masses,
  \(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}}\) an addition to \(N_{\mathrm{eff}}\),
  \(\Delta S_{\mathrm{m}}\) the change of an action under a change
  \(\Delta g^{MN}\) of the metric. In an uncertainty relation it is the
  spread, the root-mean-square deviation from the mean value in a state:
  \(\Delta x_0\), \(\Delta k_{x_0}\), \(\Delta\xi\), \(\Delta(fy)\) (as
  in the author's \(\Delta x_0\,\Delta p_{x_0} \ge \hbar/2\)). Status:
  STANDARD.
\item
  \(K\): The block variable, \(K = 1/f^2\). Unit: dimensionless. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\kappa\): The eight-dimensional gravitational coupling,
  \(\kappa = 8\pi G_8\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-6}\). Value:
  \(\kappa/V_\perp = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}}\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 2.328\times10^{-36}\)
  GeV\(^{-2}\). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\mathrm{d}\): The differential: \(\mathrm{d}X\) is an infinitesimal
  change of \(X\), \(\mathrm{d}X/\mathrm{d}Y\) the derivative of \(X\)
  with respect to \(Y\), \(\mathrm{d}^nX\) the volume element of \(n\)
  variables. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}}\): The energy density of the block stabiliser,
  a function of the block scale,
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{stab}} \propto f^{-8}\); its value at the static block
