  field of a dark-matter quantum (the lowest odd level of dirac16complex
  on the static block, in either half) and its Dirac adjoint. Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{3/2}\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Psi_{\mathrm{fold}},\ \bar\Psi_{\mathrm{fold}}\): The
  four-dimensional field of a fold-band quantum (an even zero mode of
  dirac16complex, massless) and its Dirac adjoint. Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{3/2}\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(n^{\mathrm{sc}}_{\mathrm{DF}}\): The transition scalar density
  between the dark-matter orbital and a fold-band orbital. Unit: GeV
  (normalised orbitals). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(a_{\mathrm{DM}},\ b_{\mathrm{DM}}\): The upper and lower components
  of the dark-matter orbital (block spinor \((a, \mathrm{i}b)\),
  normalised on one half). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{1/2}\). Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(a_{\mathrm{fold}},\ b_{\mathrm{fold}}\): The upper and lower
  components of a fold-band orbital (\(b_{\mathrm{fold}} = 0\) for the
  even zero mode). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{1/2}\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(G_\lambda\): The four-dimensional strength of the self-interaction
  channel of the dark matter into fold-band pairs. Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}\). Value: \(\le 2.3\times10^{-15}\) GeV\(^{-2}\)
  (for \(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} \le 0.001\)). Status: ESTIMATE.
\item
  \(H\): The author's constant (an inverse length) that fixes the period
  \(\pi/(6H)\) of \(x_0\), the Lovelock couplings, the cosmological
  constant \(\Lambda = -10H^2\) and the mass parameter \(m_\Psi = H\).
  Unit: GeV (1/length). Value: 14.296 TeV (from the fit of
  \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) and \(\rho_{\mathrm{EW},0}\)). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(\tilde I_\lambda\): The overlap of the squared transition scalar
  density,
  \(\tilde I_\lambda = H^{-1}\int_{-L}^{0}(n^{\mathrm{sc}}_{\mathrm{DF}})^2\mathrm{e}^{-3Hy}\mathrm{d}y\).
  Unit: dimensionless. Value: 14.38. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(f\): The block scale: the common scale factor of the hidden
  direction and the extra times, \(f = \mathrm{e}^{-a_4}b_4^{1/2}\) (the
  block is \(f^2\) times a four-dimensional space of constant curvature
  \(-H^2\)). Unit: dimensionless. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(v_{\mathrm{t}}\): The coordinate volume of the compact extra times
  \(x_5, x_6, x_7\). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-3}\). Value: not fixed;
  \(v_{\mathrm{t}} = H^{-3}\) in the quoted conversions. Status:
  ASSUMED.
\item
  \(L\): The depth of the regular tip: each half of a period of \(x_0\)
  ends at \(y = -L\); \(HL = 3\). Unit: length. Value: \(L = 3/H\)
  (\(fL = 8.926\times10^{-21}\) m at the stabilised block). Status:
  ASSUMED.
\item
  \(\mathrm{e}\): Euler's number, the base of the natural logarithm;
  \(\mathrm{e}^{X}\) is the exponential of \(X\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: 2.718281828. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(y\): The hidden coordinate of the \(y\) chart,
  \(y = \ln\lvert\sin z\rvert/(6H)\), with \(y = 0\) at the fold
  (\(z = \pi/2\)) and \(y = -L\) at the regular tip; each half of a
  period of \(x_0\) is the interval \(-L \le y \le 0\). Unit: length.
  Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathrm{d}\): The differential: \(\mathrm{d}X\) is an infinitesimal
  change of \(X\), \(\mathrm{d}X/\mathrm{d}Y\) the derivative of \(X\)
  with respect to \(Y\), \(\mathrm{d}^nX\) the volume element of \(n\)
  variables. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\sigma_\lambda\): The cross-section of the self-interaction channel
  (per final fold species). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}\). Status:
  ESTIMATE.
\item
  \(v_{\mathrm{rel}}\): The relative velocity of the two annihilating
  particles; \(\langle\sigma v_{\mathrm{rel}}\rangle\) the thermal
  average. Unit: dimensionless (c = 1). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(M_{\mathrm{DM}}\): The mass of a dark-matter particle (the lowest
  odd level of dirac16complex). Unit: GeV. Value: 44.35 TeV. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\pi\): The number pi, the ratio of the circumference of a circle to
  its diameter. Unit: dimensionless. Value: 3.141592654. Status:
  STANDARD.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} The contact self-interaction
\(-\frac{\lambda}{2}(\bar\Psi\Psi)^2\) of IN-5, projected on the dark
matter and the fold bands, contains the transition
\((\bar\Psi_{\mathrm{DM}}\Psi_{\mathrm{fold}})(\bar\Psi_{\mathrm{fold}}\Psi_{\mathrm{DM}})\)
with the scalar transition density \(n^{\mathrm{sc}}_{\mathrm{DF}}\) of
the two orbitals (\(b_{\mathrm{fold}} = 0\), so only
\(b_{\mathrm{DM}}a_{\mathrm{fold}}\) remains; it is odd under the fold
reflection, its square even): the dark matter annihilates into pairs of
fold quanta with the four-dimensional coupling \(G_\lambda\) and the
overlap \(\tilde I_\lambda = 14.38\) (with its factor \(1/H\)). Status:
ESTIMATE (the cross-section; the Fierz coefficients are not computed),
\(\tilde I_\lambda\) DERIVED.

\[
\frac{\mathrm{d}\big(\rho_{\mathrm{fold}}\,\mathrm{e}^{4a_4}\big)}{\mathrm{d}x_4} =
 2M_{\mathrm{DM}}\,\Gamma_\lambda\,\mathrm{e}^{4a_4},\qquad
\Gamma_\lambda = 2\,n_{\mathrm{DM,eq}}\,\bar n_{\mathrm{DM,eq}}\,\sigma_\lambda
 v_{\mathrm{rel}}\,N_{\mathrm{fold}},\qquad
\frac{2\Gamma_\lambda}{n_{\mathrm{fold,eq}}\,\mathcal{H}} \ll 1 \tag{FE-136}
\]

\textbf{Symbols in (FE-136).}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathrm{d}\): The differential: \(\mathrm{d}X\) is an infinitesimal
  change of \(X\), \(\mathrm{d}X/\mathrm{d}Y\) the derivative of \(X\)
  with respect to \(Y\), \(\mathrm{d}^nX\) the volume element of \(n\)
  variables. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\rho_{\mathrm{fold}}\): The energy density of the fold bands. Unit:
  \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathrm{e}\): Euler's number, the base of the natural logarithm;
  \(\mathrm{e}^{X}\) is the exponential of \(X\). Unit: dimensionless.
  Value: 2.718281828. Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(a_4\): The author's metric function of the time \(x_4\) that scales
  ordinary 3-space: the scale factor of cosmology is
  \(\mathrm{e}^{a_4}\). Unit: dimensionless. Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(x_4\): The time; dots denote \(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x_4\). Unit:
  length (= time, c = 1). Status: AUTHOR.
\item
  \(M_{\mathrm{DM}}\): The mass of a dark-matter particle (the lowest
  odd level of dirac16complex). Unit: GeV. Value: 44.35 TeV. Status:
  DERIVED.
\item
  \(\Gamma_\lambda\): The rate per volume of the self-interaction
  channel. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{4}\). Status: ESTIMATE.
\item
  \(n_{\mathrm{DM,eq}},\ \bar n_{\mathrm{DM,eq}}\): The equilibrium
  number densities of dark-matter particles and antiparticles of one
  species. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{3}\). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(\sigma_\lambda\): The cross-section of the self-interaction channel
  (per final fold species). Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}\). Status:
  ESTIMATE.
\item
  \(v_{\mathrm{rel}}\): The relative velocity of the two annihilating
  particles; \(\langle\sigma v_{\mathrm{rel}}\rangle\) the thermal
  average. Unit: dimensionless (c = 1). Status: STANDARD.
\item
  \(N_{\mathrm{fold}}\): The number of fold bands: the massless fold
  zero modes of \(\Psi_A\) and of \(\Psi_B\) (two Dirac fermions). Unit:
  dimensionless. Value: 2. Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(n_{\mathrm{fold,eq}}\): The number density the fold bands would have
  in thermal equilibrium. Unit: \(\mathrm{GeV}^{3}\). Status: DERIVED.
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}\): The expansion rate of ordinary 3-space,
  \(\mathcal{H} = \dot a_4\) (the Hubble rate of the observer; in the
  hot Universe
  \(\mathcal{H} = (8\pi^3g_\rho/90)^{1/2}T^2/M_{\mathrm{Pl}}\)). Unit:
  GeV. Status: DERIVED.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{The terms.} While the dark matter is in equilibrium each
annihilation puts the energy \(2M_{\mathrm{DM}}\) into the fold bands
(\(\Gamma_\lambda\) the annihilation rate per volume into all fold
species); the condition on the right keeps the fold bands out of
equilibrium. Status: DERIVED (integrated from the highest temperature to
the freeze-out):
\(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} = 1.83\times10^{26}\,G_\lambda^2\) GeV\(^4\),
so \(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} \le 0.001\) requires
\(G_\lambda \le 2.3\times10^{-15}\) GeV\(^{-2}\),
i.e.~\(\lambda \le 1.4\times10^{-10}\,H^{-6}\) (ASSUMED bound; otherwise
the fold bands thermalise and decouple as four Weyl species, FE-133).
Residual \(1.1\times10^{-5}\).

\hypertarget{the-coupled-system}{%
\section{The coupled system}\label{the-coupled-system}}

\hypertarget{which-fields-appear-in-which-equations}{%
\subsection{Which fields appear in which
equations}\label{which-fields-appear-in-which-equations}}

Every field of UM-2 obeys its own field equation and enters the
equations of the other fields as a source, a mean field, a current or a
parameter. The table lists, for each field, its own equations and the
other equations in which it appears.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3800}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4200}}@{}}

No Higgs field and no Higgs particle appear in any equation: the masses
of \(W^\pm\) and \(Z\) come from the tip sigma field (FE-51), those of
the fermions from their localisation in \(x_0\) and the tip mixing
(FE-59, FE-60).

\hypertarget{how-the-system-is-solved}{%
\subsection{How the system is solved}\label{how-the-system-is-solved}}

The coupled system is solved in eight steps. Each step solves a group of
equations exactly or with a controlled approximation, uses the results
of the earlier steps, and is checked by the residual of its solution in
the equations it uses (section ``What was verified'').

\textbf{Step 1: the background.} The background equations FE-38 to FE-40
(with the tensors FE-35 to FE-37 and the conservation law FE-41) are
solved in closed form for a static block with the stabiliser FE-4: FE-42
and FE-43 give \(K_* = 12 + \frac43\sqrt{51}\), \(f_* = 0.215556\) and
\(\mathcal{G}(K_*) = 13.8081\). Linearised (FE-44 to FE-46) they give
the stable radion (\(\omega_{\mathrm{r}} = 3.38705\,H\)) and the
adiabatic response of the block to matter (FE-47), and integrated
numerically from a deflating primordial block they show how the block
reaches the static point within \(x_4 < 1/H\) (deflation rates up to
\(\lvert\mathcal{H}_f\rvert = 2.63\,H\), the largest deflation of the
block scale \(f/f_* = 2.917\), then radion oscillations at \(3.3868\,H\)
and a dust-like expansion). The reduction FE-48, FE-49 gives
four-dimensional general relativity with
\(8\pi G_{\mathrm{N}} = \kappa/(\mathcal{G}(K_*)V_\perp)\) and
\(\Lambda_4 = 0\) at the static point. The block-averaged sources of
matter are included at first order only, with the hidden pressure
\(\bar p\); the block-shape source \(p_0 - p_5\) is not (OPEN).

\textbf{Step 2: the masses.} On the static block every mass is an
\(x_0\) eigenvalue. For the bosons FE-50 to FE-52 give the massless
graviton, photon and gluons, the tower FE-53 (with the potential FE-54)
and the \(W^\pm\), \(Z\) eigenvalues from the tip condition FE-51; FE-55
fixes \(\beta_{\mathrm{W}}\) by the measured electroweak parameter,
reproduces \(m_{\mathrm{Z}}\) from \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\), and fixes the
Kaluza-Klein scale \(m_{\mathrm{KK}} = 121.32\) TeV and with it
\(H = 14.296\) TeV. For the fermions the exact reduction FE-56 gives the
zero modes FE-57 and the tip values FE-58; the mass conditions FE-59
(one generation) and FE-60 (three generations, with the tip matrices of
FE-61) reproduce the twelve measured masses with the right-handed
localisations and the measured mixing matrices \(U\) and
\(V_{\mathrm{CKM}}\); the neutrino masses FE-100 are among them. FE-62
gives the spectrum of dirac16complex: the massless fold bands and the
dark matter at \(44.35\) TeV.

\textbf{Step 3: dirac16complex in Kohn-Sham theory and its back-reaction
on the block.} The Kohn-Sham equations FE-63 with the mean fields FE-64
(densities FE-65, energy FE-66, boundary conditions FE-69) are solved to
self-consistency for many-body states of dirac16complex on the block.
Their energy-momentum tensor FE-67 (checked by the identities FE-68) is
the source of the background equations: its block averages \(\rho\),
\(p_3\) and \(\bar p = \frac14(p_0 + 3p_5)\) enter the block response
FE-45 to FE-47, which changes \(f\) and with it every mass of step 2.
The block-shape source \(p_0 - p_5\) of these states is up to 4 times
\(\bar p\) (OPEN).

\textbf{Step 4: the portals and the condensates.} On the zero modes the
portals become the contact interactions FE-70 (I11, I13) and FE-71
(I12), with overlaps computed from the Kohn-Sham orbitals. In an
environment the levels of dirac16complex shift by the mean fields of the
Standard-Model densities and condensates (FE-72, FE-73), with the
condensates of the plasma from FE-74 to FE-78 (QCD trace anomaly,
massive vectors, order-\(\alpha\) pressure, running couplings without a
Higgs particle). Conversely the dark matter weakens the gauge couplings
and changes the \(x_0\) problem of \(W^\pm\) and \(Z\) (FE-79), while
the photon and the gluons stay massless.

\textbf{Step 5: gravity closes the loop.} In ten environments, from the
cosmic mean today to a neutron-star core and the dark-matter freeze-out
(densities FE-86), the block responds to every species through FE-80,
with the hidden pressure FE-81 of matter and of the hot plasma, the
block dependence of the gauge couplings FE-82, of the QCD scale and the
nucleon mass FE-83, FE-84, and of the fermion masses FE-85. The loop
mass, source, block, mass is iterated to self-consistency; its gain is
at most \(1.05\times10^{-33}\), so the block moves by at most
\(\Delta\ln f = 1.1\times10^{-34}\) (at the freeze-out) and by
\(7\times10^{-94}\) today.

\textbf{Step 6: the neutrino flavours.} With the masses and the tip
matrix of step 2, the four-fermion interactions FE-101, FE-102 and the
matter potentials FE-103, the flavour evolution FE-104 is solved exactly
in vacuum and in matter (FE-105 to FE-108: accelerator baselines, the
Sun, the Earth, supernova envelopes). In the early Universe the thermal
potential FE-109 delays the onset of oscillations, and the decoupling
equations FE-110 to FE-114 give \(N_{\mathrm{eff}}\) (FE-115). In dense
neutrino gases the linearised kinetic equations give the fast flavour
instability (FE-116 to FE-119). Today the neutrinos form the cosmic
neutrino background FE-120.

\textbf{Step 7: the thermal history.} With the \(x_0\) masses of step 2
the degrees of freedom FE-121 to FE-123 give the expansion of the hot
Universe. The Boltzmann equation FE-124 with the thermal average FE-125,
the cross-sections FE-127 and the unitarity bounds FE-128 fixes the I13
coupling \(G_{\mathrm{A}} = 1.278\times10^{-9}\) GeV\(^{-2}\) by the
measured relic density FE-126. The freeze-in FE-129, FE-130 bounds the
I11 coupling by the extra radiation (benchmark
\(G_{\mathrm{V}} = 4.38\times10^{-17}\) GeV\(^{-2}\) for
\(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} = 0.001\)), the self-interaction channel
FE-135, FE-136 bounds \(\lambda\), and FE-131 to FE-134 show that the
right-handed neutrinos are never thermalised with the baryon current in
I13 (with a \(B - L\) current they would add
\(\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}} = 0.144\)).

\textbf{Step 8: the linear perturbations.} The metric series FE-87 is
evaluated term by term: order 1 with \(n = 0\) is the four-dimensional
cosmological perturbation theory FE-88 to FE-90, with the species of
UM-2 (the dark matter of step 7, FE-91; baryons FE-92; photons FE-93 to
FE-95; the three neutrino mass states of step 2 and the fold bands of
step 7, FE-96), the background and primordial spectrum FE-97; it gives
the CMB, CNB and BAO, the potentials FE-98 and \(\sigma_8\) (FE-99). The
terms \(n \ge 1\) are the graviton tower of step 2, the radion term is
the block response of step 5, and order 2 is estimated.

\hypertarget{the-solution-in-concert}{%
\subsection{The solution in concert}\label{the-solution-in-concert}}

The steps share quantities: the same mass, coupling or density enters
equations of different steps. UM-2 checks 26 such links, each the
difference between the values of one quantity in two or more equations.
21 of them agree to between \(5\times10^{-16}\) and
\(1.9\times10^{-5}\), the precision of the stored numbers and of the
numerical methods. Five differ by design, each by an amount that the
results tolerate: the weak angle of the \(x_0\) masses and of the \(Z\)
widths (relative change of the total width \(8.3\times10^{-3}\), the
size of the electroweak loop corrections); the Hubble rate of the
thermal history and of four-dimensional gravity at the freeze-out
(\(1.7\times10^{-4}\): the thermal history leaves out the fold bands);
the radiation density after neutrino decoupling in the thermal history
and in the CMB computation (\(5.9\times10^{-3}\): instantaneous
decoupling in the thermal history); the plasma energy density at the
freeze-out in the thermal history and in the block sources
(\(1.3\times10^{-3}\): the trace anomaly of the interacting plasma is in
the block sources only); and the radion frequency of the linear and of
the nonlinear evolution (\(7.9\times10^{-5}\): measured from the zero
crossings of a finite oscillation).

\hypertarget{what-was-verified}{%
\section{What was verified}\label{what-was-verified}}

\hypertarget{the-checks}{%
\subsection{The checks}\label{the-checks}}

The computations of UM-2 are done by sixteen Python scripts (folder
\texttt{universe\_model\_2/code}), each of which tests its own results
against independent computations, closed forms or limits, with a stated
tolerance. There are 288 checks in all, and all pass: 235 of the
computations of version 3, 52 of the residual analysis of the solution
(\texttt{um2\_residuals.py}) and one added in version 4 for the direct
detection of the I12 benchmark (\texttt{um2\_coupled.py}). The scripts
are deterministic (the Kohn-Sham computations run with one numerical
thread), so every number of this document can be reproduced by running
them.

\hypertarget{the-residuals-of-the-solution}{%

solution}\label{the-residuals-of-the-solution}}

For 26 of the field equations the stored solution is substituted afresh
into the equation as printed in this document (or into its closed form).
The residual is the relative amount by which the equation fails; the
neglected terms are those that the approximation of the solution leaves
out. Every residual is below its tolerance.

\hypertarget{what-is-not-verified}{%
\subsection{What is not verified}\label{what-is-not-verified}}

The following are not settled and are labelled OPEN where they occur:
the block-shape mode driven by \(p_0 - p_5\) (the order-0 metric has one
block scale; its response to a block-anisotropic source is not computed,
and the source is not small compared with the hidden pressure that is
kept); the Gauss-Bonnet and cubic corrections to the graviton operator
of the Kaluza-Klein tower; the tuned value of \(\Lambda_4\) that the
observed dark energy needs; the normalisation that relates the tip
mixing matrices \(\mathcal{K}\) to the Yukawa matrices \(\mathcal{Y}\)
of the action; correlation beyond the local exchange in the Kohn-Sham
treatment of dirac16complex; and the nonlinear evolution of the fast
flavour instability. The cross-section of the self-interaction channel
(FE-135), the electroweak condensates below \(m_{\mathrm{W}}\) (FE-78),
the QCD interaction measure below 1 GeV (FE-75) and the block dependence
of the QCD scale and the nucleon mass (FE-83, FE-84) are estimates.
